### Page 2

宏观经济点评  
请务必阅读正文之后的信息披露和法律声明 2 
煤价格分别上涨 2.34%、2.69%；建材相关价格边际回升，熟料、全国水
泥均价和石油沥青价格环比分别上涨 7.90%、3.17%、9.09%，PVC 价格
上涨 3.09%，螺纹钢现货价格小幅上涨 0.36%。有色金属表现分化，铜价
环比上涨 0.27%，铝价下降 2.50%；新能源相关商品仍偏弱，碳酸锂价格
环比下降 22.33%，纯碱、光伏级多晶硅和热轧卷价格分别下降 2.92%、
0.60%、1.98%。终端食品价格整体偏弱，农产品批发价格 200 指数环比
下降 2.96%，猪肉批发价下降 3.64%，蔬菜和鸡蛋价格分别下降 8.53%、
12.57%；汽油、柴油市场价分别上涨 0.54%、5.08%。 
生产端结构分化，需求端仍受内需偏弱与假期扰动共同影响 。10 月，生
产端结构分化，长流程钢铁和部分化工行业偏弱，短流程钢铁、水泥发运
等指标边际改善；需求端受国庆假期影响较大，电影票房明显走强， 汽车
零售、快递和公路货运环比回落；地产销售同比继续走弱、二手房价格下
行，指向地产修复基础仍不牢固。价格端看，能源、化工和建材价格回
升，农产品价格继续下降，显示当前价格修复更多由上游工业品推动，尚
未形成向终端需求和消费价格的广泛传导。往后看，需重点观察假期扰动
消退后生产和物流等能否恢复，以及上游价格上涨能否在需求改善的配合
下形成更持续的价格修复。 
风险提示：国内稳增长政策推进不及预期， 地产市场修复不及预期，海外
需求超预期回落，能源及大宗商品价格波动超预期，地缘政治冲突加剧。

1. 生产端 
1.1 开工率 
截至 10 月第四周， 高炉开工率 （247 家） 为80.01%， 环比下降0.49 个百分点； 电
炉开工率为 64.10%，环比下降 0.99 个百分点；冷轧开工率为 83.02%，环比持平；螺
纹钢开工率为 35.73%， 环比下降2.75 个百分点； 磨机运转率为39.92%， 环比下降2.28
个百分点；浮法玻璃开工率为 66.75%，环比持平；石油沥青装置开工率为 23.90%，环
比上升 5.75 个百分点；PVC 开工率为 61.47%，环比下降 0.63 个百分点；纯碱开工率
为 66.55%，环比上升 2.89 个百分点；PTA 开工率为 69.01%，环比上升 2.04 个百分
点；全钢胎开工率为 57.79%，环比上升 0.16 个百分点；半钢胎开工率为 64.78%，环
比持平。

1.2 库存 
库存方面， 螺纹钢库存为147.21 万吨， 环比下降3.39%； 冷轧库存为129.50 万吨，
环比下降 0.99%；热卷库存为 360.40 万吨，环比下降 2.24%；铁矿石港口库存为
16,462.55 万吨，环比上升 0.14%；焦煤库存（247 家钢厂）为 761.14 万吨，环比下降
0.42%； 煤炭重点电厂库存可用天数为25.20 天， 环比增加9.57%； 水泥库容比为59.22%，
环比下降 0.22 个百分点； 水泥发运率为41.32%， 环比下降4.20 个百分点； 浮法玻璃库
存为 7,051.90 万重量箱，环比下降 0.72%；纯碱企业库存为 136.71 万吨，环比下降
7.88%；沥青仓库库存为 31,870 吨，环比持平。

2. 需求端 
2.1 地产 
截至 10 月第四周， 地产方面，30 城商品房成交面积为 172.90 万平方米， 环比增长
10.21%；100 城土地成交占地面积为 1,917.75 万平方米， 环比下降51.44%； 成交土地
溢价率为 1.39%，环比上升 0.72 个百分点。二手房挂牌量指数全国为 0.70，环比下降
6.64%；一线城市为 0.07，环比持平；二线城市为 0.44，环比下降 2.22%；三线城市为
1.01，环比下降 9.82%。二手房挂牌价指数全国为 139.72，环比上涨 0.04%； 一线城市
为 170.34， 环比下降0.27%； 二线城市为126.09， 环比上涨0.09%； 三线城市为120.72，
环比下降 0.01%。

投资评级说明 
投资建议的评级标准  评级 说明 
评级标准为报告发布日后的 6 个月内公司股价 （或行业
指数） 相对均值基准指数的相对市场表现。 其中A 股市
场以沪深 300 指数为基准； 新三板市场以三板成指 （针
对协议转让标的） 或三板做市指数 （针对做市转让标的）
为基准；香港市场以恒生指数为基准。 
股票评级 
买入 相对均值基准指数涨幅在 10%以上 
增持 相对均值基准指数涨幅在 5%~10%之间 
中性 相对均值基准指数涨幅在-5%~+5%之间 
减持 相对均值基准指数跌幅在 5%以上 
行业评级 
看好 相对表现优于市场 
中性 相对表现与市场持平 
看淡 相对表现弱于市场

公司簡介 
鴻海為專業 ODM/EMS 廠商，產品涵蓋手
機、伺服器、平板、網通設備等終端產品，
以及各類電子零組件如連接器、連接線等，
旗下重要子公司包含工業富聯 (601138 
SH)、富智康 (2038 HK) 及鴻騰精密 (6088 
HK)，近年公司開始積極投入研發資源在電
動車、數位醫療、機器人、AI、半導體、5G
等領域。2Q26 產品營收占比：消費暨智能
29%、雲端網路 51%、電腦終端 15%、元
件及其他 5%。

主要客戶 ：Apple、HP、Dell、Sony、Cisco、
北美四大 CSP 等 
主要競爭對手：

黃璽銘 hsiming.huang@sionopac.com 
本次報告：電話訪談

鴻海 (2317 TT) 
上修獲利效益預期

永豐觀點 
垂直整合能力，維持 AI 伺服器領先地位，營收規模擴大，上修獲利效益預期。 
 投資評價與建議 
研究部維持鴻海買進建議：鴻海集團於科技日展現垂直整合、精密加工能力，並憑
藉著規模化交付能力， 預期於伺服器市場維持四成以上市占率 、並能優先取得新世
代產品參考設計， 營收規模放大的同時體現獲利率效益， 獲利金額擴大以及營運資
金運用，並樹立組裝產業進入門檻。研究部參考近期公告之財務及產業資訊，預期
鴻海 2H26~2027 年營運具上修空間 ， 調整後預估鴻海 2026 年/2027 年稅後 EPS 
18.79 元/25.46 元。惟近期總體利率環境影響股價評價，目標本益比從 15 倍調整
至 14 倍，維持鴻海買進建議，目標價 356 元(14x2027FEPS)。

ESG 評析 
鴻海企業永續評鑑整體為 A 等，於 SASB 產業永續議題與跨產業五大永續面向平
均得分分別為 A 等與 A 等。

### Page 2

2

鴻海為專業 ODM/EMS 廠商，產品涵蓋手機、伺服器、平板、網通設備等終端產
品，以及各類電子零組件如連接器、連接線等，旗下重要子公司包含工業富聯
(601138 SH)、富智康(2038 HK)及鴻騰精密(6088 HK)，近年公司開始積極投入研
發資源在電動車 、 數位醫療 、 機器人 、AI、 半導體 、5G 等領域 。 主要客戶為Apple、
HP、Dell、Sony、Cisco、北美四大 CSP 等。2Q26 產品營收占比：消費暨智能
29%、雲端網路 51%、電腦終端 15%、元件及其他 5%。 
鴻海科技日展現垂直整合能力  統合零組件到機櫃生產：(1) 鴻海科技日強調展現
垂直整合能力及精密加工能力，提供客戶 one stop shop，垂直整合、設計製造服
務。鴻海具備伺服器 Level 1 到 Level 12 的生產能力，從螺絲、零組件、機構件
等至伺服器系統整機櫃。鴻海團隊從產品設計開始即整合組裝、良率、服務零組件
到整系統最佳化的解決方案。(2) 鴻海協助輝達、Google 打造 AI 工廠、AI 基礎
建設，以自動化縮短資料中心建造時程。(3) 施耐德電機、東元提供電力設備，與
鴻海進一步合作打造模組化資料中心。 4. 本田與三菱電機展示與鴻海在電動車未
來自動駕駛、駕駛監控系統、 ECU 等合作方向。 (5) 鴻海同時推出新電動車平
台,Model A+ 、K 系列。A+為休旅車，其特色為取消 B 柱讓車內寬敞，並將配置 
L4 自動駕駛感測器。Model K 為輕型卡車，提供短程 120 公里、長程 250 公里車
型選擇。 
展場上鴻海集團展出 AI 伺服器相關產品，1. 與 Vertiv 合作液冷基建的 AI Data 
Hall 解決方案。(2) 展出 NVIDIA DSX POD 解決方案，以及 Vera Rubin 世代 
Compute Tray、Switch Tray、CPO Switch、CPU Tray、LPU Tray 等相關產品。
(3) Google 下一代 TPU 將使用的 OCS 全光交換機。(4) 在 NVIDIA CPO Switch
使用 的精 密 光學 對位 機 構 件、FAU 光纖 連 接產 品。(5) 集團 公司 鴻 騰精密
(6088.HK)， 展出液冷相關零組件QD、Manifold； 電力相關零組件Busbar、Power 
Whip；主板上連接器 Paladin、SOCAMM 等。甚至下一代 Vera Rubin Ultra 將
採用 ZQD、POGO 連接器也已取得 NPI。 
美系大廠資本支出 2027 年上修至 6 成以上增長 建置需求未歇：上週美股法說會
後，統計 Bloomberg 美系五大 CSP 加上 Space X，2026 年資本支出從年增率從
分別從 102%上修至 103%，2027 年從 44%上修至 60%，2026 年及 2027 年資
本支出金額分別達 8,793 億美元及 1.41 兆美元，顯示AI 算力建置需求仍未停歇。 
上修 3Q26、4Q26 營收獲利預估：鴻海先前公告 3Q26 營收達 3.0 兆優於預期，
以及子公司工業富聯公告 3Q26 財報優於預期。以此推算鴻海營收擴大規模效益
帶來獲利表現上修，研究部預期營益率增長至 3.8%，稅後 EPS 上修至 5.10 元。
上修 4Q26 營收預期，主因3Q26 營運基期墊高，以及iPhone 維持出貨動能、GB
機櫃出貨量出貨量、Vera Rubin 機櫃單價跳升等，推算帶動營收季增 14%，稅後
EPS 上修至 5.85 元。

營運現況與分析

### Page 3

3

AI 伺服器需求為鴻海營運增長動能：AI 伺服器需求主要由美系 CSP、AI 模型商及
Neocloud 驅動，鴻海 GPU AI 伺服器除了原本美系 CSP 需求，也將有新型雲端
客戶開始貢獻。研究部預期鴻海維持 VR200 的主要供應商角色，整體市占率維持
達 40%以上，近期上修預估整體市場 Vera Rubin 至約 64,000 櫃 NVL72 (市場預
期為 80,000~90,000 櫃)，同時鴻海 2027 年將增加美系 CSP AI ASIC 伺服器機櫃
供應，為 2027 年營運增長動能，鴻海在提高市占率、營收規模放大的同時體現獲
利率效益、獲利金額擴大以及營運資金運用，並能樹立組裝產業進入門檻。 
綜合以上，上修後預估 2026 年營收 11.1 兆，年增 37%，毛利率 6.0%，營益率
3.7%，稅後淨利 2,627 億，年增 39%，稅後 EPS 18.79 元。2027 年營收 15.5 兆
元，年增 39%，毛利率 5.4%，營益率 3.4%，稅後淨利 3,555 億元，年增 35%，
稅後 EPS 25.46 元。

### Page 1

產業速報  
                                        2026 年 10 月 30 日

1 群益投顧 
國巨 (2327 TT) 整體 3Q26 財報表現優異，其中營收為 495.3 億元 (QoQ +11.4% 、 YoY 
+49.7%) ，主因受惠 AI 帶 動 相 關 被 動 元 件 增 溫 ， 其 中AI 占比較 2Q26 的 16% 提升至
近 20% ； EPS 5.83 元，優於 預期 。 然 展 望 4Q26 ，受 PC 市 場 疲 軟 及 消 費 性 電 子 需 求
放緩抵銷預期 AI 於 4Q 帶 來 的 強 勁 增 長，國 巨 4Q26 營收將從預估季增雙位數下修至
微 幅 季 增，不 過 因 VR 將於 2027 年 正 式 放 量，被 動 產 能 供 需 將 會 更 緊，故 維 持 整 體 被
動 元 件 漲 價 趨 勢 向 上 ， 看 法 為 短 空 長 多 。

研 究 員 ： 葉 芷 瑄   
vanessa.yeh@cim.capital.com.tw

國巨 3Q26 法 說 會 重 點：  
國巨 (2327 TT) 於昨 (29) 舉辦 3Q26 法 說 會 ， 整 體 3Q26 財 報 表 現 優 異 ， 其 中
營收為 495.3 億元 (QoQ +11.4% 、 YoY +49.7%) ， 主 因 受 惠 AI 帶 動 相 關 被 動
元 件 增 溫 ， 其 中 AI 占比較 2Q26 的 16% 提 升 至 近 20% ； 毛 利 率 因 產 品 組 合
轉佳至 41.0%(QoQ +2.5ppts 、 YoY +4.8ppts) ； OPM 29.5%(QoQ +3.1ppts 、
YoY +6.6ppts) ； 歸 屬 母 公 司 稅 後 淨 利 為 119.8 億元 (QoQ +27.2% 、 YoY 
+88.5%) ；EPS 5.83 元 ， 優 於 群 益 預 估 的 5.15 元 。 然 展 望 4Q26 ，受 PC 市場
疲 軟 及 消 費 性 電 子 需 求 放 緩 抵 銷 預 期 AI 於 4Q 帶 來 的 強 勁 增 長 ，BB ratio
略 較 前 季 呈 現 微 幅 下 滑，國 巨 4Q26 營 收 將 從 預 估 季 增 雙 位 數 下 修 至 微 幅 季
增 ， 不 過 因 VR 將於 2027 年 正 式 放 量 ， 被 動 產 能 供 需 將 會 更 緊 ， 故 維 持 整
體 被 動 元 件 漲 價 趨 勢 向 上 ， 看 法 為 短 空 長 多 。

2 群益投顧 
  【 投 資 評 等 說 明 】 
  評等  定義  
  強力買進 (Strong Buy)  首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間≥ 35%  
  買進 (Buy)  15%≤ 首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間<35%  
  區間操作 (Trading Buy)  5%≤ 首 次 評 等 潛 在 上 漲 空 間<15%

中立 (Neutral)  
無 法 由 基 本 面 給 予 投 資 評 等 
預 期 近 期 股 價 將 處 於 盤 整 
建 議 降 低 持 股

【 投 資 評 等 調 整 機 制 】 
  投 資 評 等 係「 首 次 」給 予 特 定 個 股 投 資 評 等 時，其 前 一 日 收 盤 價 相 較3 個月
目 標 價 之 潛 在 上 漲 空 間 計 算 而 得 。 個 股 投 資 評 等 分 為四 個 等 級 ， 定 義 如 上 。
爾 後 的 投 資 評 等係 依 循 「 首 次 評 等 」 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。  
「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」及 「 區 間 操 作 」均有 upside 目 標 價 。差 別 僅 在 於 ，
首 次 評 等 時 潛 在 報 酬 率 不 同 。 「 中 立 」 則 無 目 標 價 。 
一 旦 我 們 給 予 特 定 個 股「 強 力 買 進 」、「 買 進 」或「 區 間 操 作 」之 投 資 評 等，
就 是 責 任 的 開 始，爾 後 將 透 過 各 式 研 究 報 告 作 定 期 性、持 續 性 追 蹤 基 本 面 及
股 價 變 化 ， 直 到 停 止 推 薦 。 
停 止 推 薦 情 境 ： 
1.  達 目 標 價 。 
2.  雖 未 達 原 訂 目 標 價 ， 但 檢 視 基 本 面 、 訊 息 面 、 籌 碼 面 等 多 方 訊 息 ， 研
判 股 價 上 漲 空 間 已 然 有 限 ， 將 適 時 出 具 降 評 報 告 。 
3.  推 薦 後 股 價 不 漲 反 跌 。

【免責聲明】  
  本 研 究 報 告 僅 提 供 予 特 定 人 之 客 戶 作 為 參 考 資 料「 非 經 同 意 不 得 轉 載 」 。我
們 並 不 確 保 此 資 訊 的 完 整 性 與 正 確 性，投 資 人 應 了 解，報 告 中 有 關 未 來 預 測
之 陳 述 可 能 不 會 實 現，因 而 不 應 被 依 賴。而 且 此 報 告 並 非 根 據 特 定 投 資 目 的
或 依 預 定 對 象 之 財 務 狀 況 所 撰 寫 出 來 的，因 此，此 研 究 報 告 的 目 的，既 非 對
投 資 人 於 買 賣 證 券、選 擇 權、期 貨 或 其 他 證 券 相 關 之 衍 生 性 商 品 提 供 詢 價 服
務，亦 非 作 為 進 行 交 易 的 要 約。投 資 人 應 注 意 到 相 關 證 券 之 價 值 及 收 益，可
能會有無預警地上升或下降，產生投資回報金額可能比原始投資來得少的
情形。

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: ASE disclosed a strong set of quarterly results and guidance 
on 29 October, both beating market expectations, thanks to the strong AI 
scaling which is just getting stronger to benefit ASE’s leading-edge 
advanced packaging (LEAP) business. We raise our forecasts and 12M TP 
and reaffirm our Outperform (2) call on the stock; accumulate into any 
technical pullback.

What’s the impact: Results and guidance all beat. ASE reported 3Q26 
net profit of c.TWD27bn, beating our/consensus estimates by 25%/18% 
thanks to higher semiconductor assembly & test (SAT, or IC ATM by 
company definition) revenue which came in at TWD151bn (USD4.7bn), 7% 
above our estimate, for higher gross margin (GM) of 30.5%, 1.9pp above 
ours and topping ASE’s structural target of 24-30%. The strength was 
amplified further by lower non-operating losses, resulting in the strong 
bottom-line beat (table on p.2). Guidance was another beat thanks to the 
strong SAT business where ASE expects revenue to grow 11-13% QoQ for 
GM to top its record of 30.5% during the COVID upturn. We raise our 2026-
28E EPS by 14-24% assuming its SAT GM to reach 33% through 2027.

Strong AI gets stronger. Discussions centred on ASE’s LEAP business, 
where management further hiked revenue target this year beyond USD4bn 
(USD1.6bn in 2025), after 3 hikes YTD, thanks to strong demand for its on-
substrate (oS) packaging and initial ramp of its full-process solutions. With 
addition of new co-packaged optics (CPO) solutions, ASE expects the 
LEAP revenue to double YoY in 2027. As LEAP is margin-accretive, ASE 
expects the SAT GM to continue improving beyond 4Q26, making the 
ceiling of its structural GM target now as the floor to kick off 2027 for a 
strong start. Given management’s emphasis on “friendly pricing”, we expect 
likely continued cost passthrough across the supply chain to add to GM 
upside. Working hard to narrow the supply/demand gap, ASE further raised 
this year’s capex to USD11.5bn (from USD10.5bn), reinforcing our view 
that AI is scaling strongly to accelerate the chip industry growth.

What we recommend: Given our forecast revisions, we raise our 12M TP 
for ASE to TWD710 (previous: TWD550), based on our revised ROE-
adjusted PBR target of 5.8x (previous: 5.2x) 2027E BVPS due to a higher 
ROE trajectory. Our revised TP translates into 24x 4-quarter forward PER, 
which we see as justifying the rich PBR target given its 2026-28E EPS 
CAGR of 25%. We reiterate our Outperform (2) call with key downside risks 
of any “silicon inflation” and geopolitical overhang leading to AI capex cut.

How we differ: Our 2026-27E EPS are 5-10% above the consensus as we 
believe the street has yet to factor in its stronger LEAP business.

29 October 2026

Entering an even stronger earnings cycle (III)

➢ 3Q26 results and 4Q26 guidance all beat expectations 
➢ Strong AI scaling is getting even stronger to benefit LEAP 
➢ Reaffirm Outperform (2) call with a higher 12M TP of TWD710

Company profile 
Established in April 2018, ASE Industrial Holding Co (HoldCo) is a holding company which privatized 
ASE and SPIL fully on 30 April 2018 through equity and cash swap, respectively, and listed on the same 
day with senior management assigned mainly by ASE. HoldCo has become the strong No.1 OSAT 
maker in the world, offering a comprehensive product portfolio to its customers from wirebonding, flip-
chip & bumping, system-in-package (SiP) to broad-based electronics manufacturing services (EMS).

### Page 5

5

ASE Technology Holding (3711 TT): 29 October 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 5 Feb 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 2
ASE’s Board of Directors consists of nine members with diverse professional backgrounds.
Three of the directors are independent, representing 33 percent of the board. The Chairman also
serves as the Chief Executive Officer, resulting in unified leadership and centralized decision-
making. To maintain effective governance and oversight under this structure, ASE relies on its
independent directors and established board processes. The board currently includes one
female director, and the company continues to pursue long-term goals related to board diversity
and balanced representation. ASE has an Audit Committee, Compensation Committee, Risk
Management Committee, and Chief Government Officer to conduct effective supervision.
Capital management 3
In the past 5 years, ASE paid over 50% or nearly 50% of its earnings in dividends. However, the
dividend trend was volatile in dollar and payout ratio terms. We forecast its dividend payout ratio
to average over 50% in the next 2 years.
Related party &
transaction 2
ASE undertakes related-party transactions with its subsidiaries on sales and purchases. The
total related-party transaction sales/purchase amounts were less than 10% of its total
sales/purchases, and the transaction terms with related parties were normal compared with
those with other customers/suppliers. Thus, we do not think related-party transactions have a
meaningful impact on overall operations.
S Supply chain management 2
The supply of raw materials has the most direct impact on ASE's operations and manufacturing.
To lower overall supply-chain risks, ASE has expanded the scope of sustainability risk
management to non-tier-1 suppliers. ASE currently has over 600 non-tier-1 suppliers.
ASE requires its suppliers to comply with its Supplier Code of Conduct, which aims to ensure
that suppliers conduct business in a manner that meets labour, health & safety, environment,
business ethics & management, and various corporate compliance standards.
E Water & wastewater management 2
ASE has adopted 3 water-use strategies of reduce, reuse and recycle. Through its water
management measures, it reduced water consumption density (water consumption/revenue) by
43% in 2024 vs. that of 2015. ASE is committed to increasing water reuse at its facilities in order
to improve water efficiency.
E
Waste & hazardous
materials
management
1
ASE is committed to improving waste recycling and reuse through the 5 key elements of a
circular economy of: direct recycling, reuse, off-site regeneration, renewable materials, and
supply and rental. The recycling rate of general waste was 90% in 2024 and its hazardous waste
density declined by 53% in 2024 vs. that of 2015.
E GHG emissions 1
ASE is committed to achieving net zero emissions by advancing low carbon energy adoption,
developing smart grid technologies, and strengthening high tech energy management systems.
As of 2024, 19% of total manufacturing energy consumption comes from renewable sources,
88% of ASE facilities use renewable energy, and 10 sites have achieved RE100 certification.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

ASE Technology Holding (3711 TT): 29 October 2026

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: Following our results memo, we fine-tune our forecasts for 
Realtek. We believe its near-term demand-rebalancing hiccups have come 
to an end, thanks to its pricing power and its ability to pass on supply-chain 
cost inflation. Given our interpretation of management’s tone that 4Q26 is 
likely to be sequentially flat, we expect Realtek’s business to return to its 
seasonal track from 1Q27. We expect it to continue capitalising on its 
structure growth driver of multi-year/multi-G (gigabits per second) 
connectivity bandwidth upgrade cycle, with any AI penetration adding to 
upside. We reaffirm Outperform (2) call with a higher 12M TP of TWD798.

What’s the impact: Forecast revisions. We fine-tune our 2026-28E EPS 
given that fundamentals are broadly intact heading into 2027. Recall 
management’s commentary at its results call (29 October) of expecting 
4Q26 to be a “prudent and steady” quarter. We interpret this as likely a 
sequentially flat revenue, counter-seasonal after its 3Q26 miss on deeper 
demand rebalancing. Without quantifying its 2027 business outlook, 
management expects the year to be one of steady growth. We forecast 
Realtek’s 2027 revenue to grow by 18% YoY, assuming its business returns 
to seasonal track after 2 years of demand rebalancing hiccups caused by 
customers’ unusual restocking and pre-stocking activities.

Back on track after recent hiccups. After 2 years of anti-seasonal 
performance on customer pre-stocking amid uncertainty over Trump’s 
reciprocal tariffs, followed by component price hikes and supply-chain 
uncertainty related to AI cannibalisation, we expect Realtek’s business to 
return to its seasonal track from 1Q27. We believe it should continue to 
capitalise on its secular driver of multi-year/multi-G bandwidth upgrade 
cycle from both wireline and wireless connectivity, including 2.5G/5G/10G 
Ethernet, 10GPON, managed switches and WiFi 7/8. Its penetration into AI, 
from cloud AI companion chips to edge AI to physical AI, should add to 
growth upside, although any meaningful contribution may take time, 
evidenced by its new product offering/development such as SSD, Ethernet 
hub, optical cable, i3C security and SerDes controllers, to name a few. 
According to management, AI will contribute a high single-digit % revenue 
this year, ramping to over 30% contribution by 2030.

What we recommend: Following forecast revisions, we raise our 12M TP 
for Realtek to TWD798 (previous: TWD750), on the same 4-quarter forward 
PER target of 20x. We reaffirm our Outperform (2) rating with key downside 
risk of any component price hikes leading to worse-than-expected demand.

How we differ: Our 2026-28E EPS are broadly in line with consensus, 
ranging from -3% to +2%.

3 November 2026

Back on track after rebalancing

➢ Fine-tuning our forecasts on demand rebalancing after pre-stocking 
➢ Likely back on seasonal track after this year’s hiccups 
➢ Reaffirming Outperform (2) call with a higher 12-month TP of TWD798

Company profile 
Realtek focuses on research, design, development and distribution of integrated circuits (ICs) for 
applications across networking & communication (NC), computer & peripheral (CP) and multimedia & 
consumer (MM). The majority of its revenue comes from NC products, including WiFi, Bluetooth, 
Ethernet and switch controllers. It also provides diversified CP products such as audio codec, IP cam, 
and solid-state drive (SSD) controllers as well as MM products like TV SoC and monitor controllers.

### Page 5

5

Realtek Semiconductor (2379 TT): 3 November 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 29 Jan 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 2
Realtek’s Board of Directors consists of nine members whose expertise spans industry,
academia, finance, accounting, and management, providing the company with a broad and
diverse range of professional perspectives. Three of these directors are independent,
representing 33 percent of the board and exceeding the regulatory minimum requirement of 20
percent for independent directors. This higher proportion of independent directors enhances
oversight, helps safeguard shareholder interests, and supports more objective decision‑making.
However, an important governance risk remains in the area of board diversity. The board
currently includes only one female director out of nine members, which represents 11 percent of
the total. This limited female representation may constrain the breadth of viewpoints and
experiences reflected in board discussions and could be perceived as misaligned with evolving
stakeholder expectations and best practices on gender diversity in corporate governance.
Capital management 1
In the past 8 years, Realtek's dividend payout ratios were above 80%. As a fabless company, it
does not need high capital expenditure for manufacturing sites. Realtek's business has shown
stable earnings growth over its 30-year history. Thus, we see its dividend policy with a high
payout ratio as appropriate.
Related party &
transaction 2
Realtek’s related-party transactions are primarily with G.M.I. Technology Inc. and Greatek
Electronics Inc. Realtek pays certain processing costs to related parties. These payments are
made under standard commercial terms and conditions. Overall, we do not observe any
significant impact on Realtek’s business arising from these related-party transactions.
S Supply chain management 2
Realtek has formulated a set of supplier management regulations in accordance with Realtek's
Supplier Sustainability Management Policy. It requires suppliers to obtain ISO9001 quality
management system certification to ensure that all of its suppliers possess a high level of
environment awareness.
E Water & wastewater management 1
As a fabless company without manufacturing plants, Realtek does not produce much wastewater
compared to manufacturing companies, and thus has a relatively limited impact on the
environment. Realtek has implemented a water conservation plan to increase water efficiency,
such as the reuse of rainwater, re-circulating water in its cooling tower and installing water-
saving devices. Its water saving rate (=reduced water consumption/total water withdrawn)
increased gradually.
E
Waste & hazardous
materials
management
1
The company reports to the Executive Yuan's Environmental Protection Administration waste
and reporting management system every month on the quantity of waste and its method of
disposal. In 2024, it produced 26.10 tonnes of industrial waste, maintaining over a 90% recycling
rate for hazardous industrial waste.
E GHG emissions 1
Realtek has independently conducted GHG emission inventory and management at its Hsinchu
Headquarters since 2019. In 2022, Realtek implemented a comprehensive ISO 14064-1:2018
GHG inventory and passed third-party verification. As of 2024, the company has completed
GHG emission inventory and third-party verification for all sites within the Realtek Group.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

營收走勢圖

研究員/吳大任 TERRY.WU@uni-psg.com

研究部對創意的投資建議維持強力買進，係因:(1)3Q26 營收、毛利
率、營業利益率皆優於預期，(2)4Q26 雖因為 ECB 將送件未給財測，
但客戶對 CPU 需求來看仍持續上修 ，(3)北美車用晶片有望在 2Q27 初
導入 ， 中國車用客戶晶片市佔率持續提升 ， 且持續拓展海外客戶 ，27-
28 年貢獻幅度大幅跳升 ，(4)晶片大廠持續上修 CPU 與加速器的配適
性，更有效率的使用 GPU 之效能，(5)不僅 200 系列晶片，CSP 客戶
的 300 系列晶片創意也有所著墨。綜合以上，上修創意 26 年 EPS 預
估為 63.3 元(原估 54.76 元，+125%YOY)，同步上修 27 年 EPS 預估
為 245.9 元(原估 214.5 元，+288.5%YOY)， 及上修28 年EPS 為 467.7
元(原估 392.6 元，+95.9%YOY，以股本膨脹 3%計算)。考量 27 年下
半年創意的成長動能更加明確上修 ， 且費用控管十分優異 ， 客戶從雲
端 AI 延伸至終端 AI，CPU、加速器、自駕晶片及 CPO 皆有著墨，故
對創意看法更加樂觀，故先前 8600 元目前價到價後，仍維持強力買
進投資建議，目標價調升至 9850 元(27 年 EPS*40XPER OR 28 年
EPS*21XPER)。

重點摘要： 
1. 3Q26 營收大幅優於預期，OPM 更加優化：  
創意 3Q26 營收達到創新高且優於上修後的預期的 187.5 億
元，成長35%QOQ，成長117.7%YOY，其中量產營收達到166.7
億元，成長44%QOQ，推估來自於CPU 的貢獻較大，且在2H26
創意也有新增一些產能支援 ， 且NRE 也達到預估的區間上緣 ，
僅衰退 11%QOQ。 毛利率達到20.59%， 優於研究部預期的19%，
更優於市場預估的 19-20%，推估主要是出貨的晶圓良率更加
提升所致。費用控制非常良好，3Q26 費用僅 13.09 億元，低
於 2Q26 的 13.18 億元，但營收卻成長35%QOQ，故使得OPM 提
升至 13.6%，大幅優於先前預估的11%。稅後淨利達到創新高
的 22.73 億元(原估 16.02 億元)，成長 46.1%QOQ，成長
162.1%YOY，EPS 16.96 元(原估 11.96 元)。

2. 因 ECB 將送件，故無 4Q26 財測，但仍正向看待：  
因為創意的 ECB 即將送件，故此次財報後無法給出財測，但
研究部仍認為 4Q26 創意營運動能將上修，主要係因先前在上
半年所提到的投片保有量外，仍有多爭取的 WAFER，從創意
3Q26 每個月的量產營收就可以觀察到持續增加，單月量產營
收已經提升至 65 億元，預估 4Q26 將能持續穩定攀升。另外
NRE 方面，CSP 下一代 CPU 將預估在 4Q26 TAPE OUT，NRE 預

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

估將翻倍成長 。 綜合以上 ，因為量產營收持續優於預期， 故上
修 4Q26 營收為 244.2 億元(原估 236 億元)，成長30.2%QOQ，
成長 97%YOY，稅後淨利上修預估為續創新高的 30.1 億元(原
估 25.35 億元)，成長32.5%QOQ，成長159.6%YOY，EPS 22.46
元(原估 18.92 元)。

3. CPU在Agentic AI世代重要性持續提升： 
根據INTEL的資料中心與人工智慧白皮書顯示，CPU 容量不足
可能造成編排卡頓、工具執行延遲、驗證循環變慢，導致GPU
在等待控制平面 （代理編排） 處理完成時利用率不足 。 若要將
代理型工作負載最佳化，就必須增加 CPU 數量，作為控制與
編排層，在兼顧 新式 AI 工作流程分散且多步驟本質的同
時，將資料高效提供給加速器。故根據INTEL實測顯示，在模
型大小為8B規模時 ， 建議加速器與CPU配比為1.4:1， 模型大小
擴大到70B時，建議配比為1:1。故在AMD簡報也顯示，2025-
2030年CPU產值規模將從260億美元提升至2030年的2200億美
元，CAGR達到50%。創意的CSP客戶所ASIC走到第八代後，CPU
與TPU的配置從先前1:4確實提升至1:2，並且全數從先前的
X86 BASE更改成自家研發的ARM BASE CPU。而CSP客戶第八代
產品放量時間點將落在4Q26末，2027年完整貢獻 ， 故從創意量
產營收看出 ， 因為配比提升CSP客戶對CPU需求持續增加 ， 故上
修CPU26年貢獻創意營收從12億美元上修至13-15億美元。在
27年方面，有鑑於客戶對於加速器及CPU的配飾性相對於第七
代提升一倍，加上下一代CPU將在4Q26 TAPE OUT，有望在2H27
開始貢獻。因為二代產品單價提升，故上修CPU貢獻營收程度
從35億美元上修35-40億美元。 
CSP二代CPU將有望在28年完整貢獻，ASP提升幅度較大，且三
代CPU目前已經開始著手研發 ， 故研究部認為 ，CPU對加速器的
配置開始往1:1走後，創意目前在客戶需求延續性強，且持續
有拿到次世代案件下，28年CPU貢獻幅度將達到50%YOY增長。

圖一、INTEL 資料中心白皮書內容

圖二、AMD 對 SERVER CPU 產值的預估

資料來源：AMD，統一研究部整理

車用將成為 27 年開始的新成長支柱 
(1)北美客戶： 
根據供應鏈訪查 ， 北美車用客戶晶片量產時間點將落在2Q26，
創意目前在 5 代晶片進度仍優於競爭對手，故以目前規劃的
投片量來看 ， 研究部維持先前預估貢獻約12 億美金營收不變 。
但根據北美客戶法說，該客戶希望將5 代晶片在 2027 年 4 月
導入機器人使用 ， 故研究部目前僅估算車用的部分 ， 尚未估計
機器人，若該客戶順利量產機器人，則貢獻創意營收有望上
修。 
(2)中國客戶： 
以中國客戶自行揭露的資料來看，全系列征程系列晶片出貨
套數已經高達 1000 萬套，在 26 年 9 月，該客戶的 CEO 在社
群媒體上表示，公司智駕晶片出貨量已達 1500 萬套。另外客
戶給出 26 年征程系列晶片將出貨 500 萬套，27 年將更達到
700 萬套的目標。先前預估 27 年以客戶出貨目標 700 萬套的
50%，350 萬套第 7 代征程晶片做估計，貢獻營收將達到 15-
20 億美元，此預估沒有變化，靜待客戶完成年報公告後再行
調整。

圖三、中國自駕車晶片公司 CEO 微博

資料來源：WEIBO，統一研究部整理

### Page 4

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  4

兩大車用客戶對創意來說 ，27年僅是開端 ， 首先北美客戶27年
才會導入5代晶片，且是機器人及無人計程車皆會導入，附帶
一提，先前展示的飛天跑車也是搭載5代晶片，故認為在客戶
量產機器人及無人計程車後，晶片需求在 28年將會超過翻倍
成長 。 另外在中國客戶方面， 除了本土市場外 ， 該公司積極拓
展海外市場，7代晶片LIFECYCLE有望比6代更久，28年目前也
以翻倍的狀況預估。

4. CSP客戶三代晶片仍有著墨機會： 
北美另一雲端客戶在10月初表示，200系列加速器已經實現量
產，該客戶已經在各地區DATA CENTER 部屬200系列晶片，而
200系列晶片後段為創意打造，推估LIFE CYCLE出貨量為數十
萬顆 ， 貢獻營收約3-5億美元 。 而在300系列方面 ， 先前市場傳
出主要供應商為美國晶片同業 ， 後來研究部訪查 ， 創意在27年
亦有BOOKING 2nm產品，而300系列晶片由N2P節點打造，故推
估創意亦有部分300系列晶片相關生意，故仍有營收貢獻。而
目前推估400系列晶片有望重回創意，挹注 28年後的營運動
能。

5. 26、27年上修預估，28年持續高度成長： 
創意3Q26營收已經大幅優於預期 ， 加上毛利率擊敗市場預期 ，
另外費用控管得宜 ， 使得3Q26 OPM不減反增 ， 另外4Q26雖然未
給財測，但在產能有所支持加上CPU需求持續推升下，營收也
略有上修，故上修創意26年營收預估為685.2億元(原估648.6
億元)，成長100.7%YOY，稅後淨利上修預估為84.8億元(原估
73.28億元)， 成長125%YOY，EPS上修預估為63.3元(原估54.76
元)。 展望27年，CPU貢獻性持續上修 ， 另外也維持車用客戶的
預估值，另外本來就有CSP客戶的200系列加速器開始大幅量
產，300系列目前有所著墨，故上修27年營收預估為2458.5億
元(原估2328億元)，成長258.8%YOY，稅後淨利上修預估為
329.5億元(原估287.5億元)， 成長288.5%YOY，EPS上修預估為
245.9元(原估214.5元)。最後，雖然目前28年投片等尚未確
定 ， 不過創意在CSP二代CPU、 中國及北美車用晶片 ， 接續世代
晶片皆有持續獲得新世代案件 ， 另外在CSP ASIC方面 ， 目前有
將從整合封裝能力開始切入 ，CSP 300/400系列晶片皆有斬獲 ，
故研究 部粗估28年創意營收將上修預估為 5145.8億元(原估
4627.5億元)，成長109.3%YOY，稅後淨利預估上修為645.5億
元(原估542億元)，成長95.9%YOY，EPS預估為467.7元(原估
392.6元，以膨脹後3%股本計算)。

附錄 一 ： 評 等 之 標 準 
評等  定義  
強力買進     預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬超過 25%以上  
買進  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬 10% 以上  
中立  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬介於 10～-10%  
降低持股  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬介於 (-10%)以下  
未評等  沒有足夠的基本資料判斷該公司評等

資 標 的 之 要 約 、 宣 傳 或 引 誘 等 事 項 。 對 於 本 投 資 報 告 所 討 論 或 建 議 之 任 何 證 券 、 投

### Page 1

삼성바이오로직스 (207940/KS) 
3Q26 Pre: 파업과 환율 부담에도 컨센 부합 
2026-10-06

제약/바이오. 이선경 / seonkyoung.lee@sks.co.kr / 3773-9089

매수(유지) 
목표주가: 2,000,000 원(유지) 
현재주가: 1,354,000 원 
상승여력: 47.7%

Signal: 파업과 환율 부담에도 컨센 부합 전망  
Key: 5 공장 매출 본격화, 미국 공장 매출 기여에 따른 성장 기대   
Step: 단기 불확실성보다는 중장기적 성장 방향성에 주목

3Q26 Pre: 파업과 환율 부담에도 성장지속, 컨센 부합 전망   
26년 3분기 연결기준 매출액은 1조 3,741억원(+9.3%YoY, +4.0%QoQ), 영업이
익은 6,098억원(-3.7%YoY, +4.0%QoQ, OPM 44.4%)으로 컨센서스 매출액 1
조 3,450억원과 영업이익 6,063억원에 부합하는 실적을 전망한다.  
5월 파업 영향으로 약 20개 배치 생산분(약 1,500억원)에 대한 원가 손실이 인식되
고 3분기 평균 환율이 전분기 대비 약 5% 하락했음에도 불구하고, 3분기부터 매출로 
인식되는 5공장 PPQ 배치 및 미국 공장의 매출 기여로 지속적인 성장이 예상된다. 약 
1,500억원 규모의 생산 차질분은 고객사와 일정 조율을 통해 출하될 예정으로 이에 
따른 4분기 매출 성장을 기대해 볼 수 있다.   
신규 수주 회복과 모달리티 확장에 주목 
시장에서 우려했던 신규 수주는 관세 이슈로 고객사의 컨택이 감소하면서 올해 저조했
으나 4 월 Section 232 결과 발표 이후 논의가 회복 단계에 있어 연내 추가 신규 수주 
가능성은 열려 있다.  
항체 시장의 경쟁심화와 차세대 모달리티 확장에 대응하기 위해 결정한 폴리펩타이드
의 인수는 연내 마무리될 예정이다. 폴리펩타이드의 2028 년 목표 EBITDA 마진은 
25%로 동사 대비 낮은 상황이나, 펩타이드 기반 CDMO 시장 성장에 따른 마진 개선
도 가능하다. 현재 임단협, 환율, 미국 공장 및 폴리펩타이드 인수에 따른 회계 영향 등 
불확실성이 큰 상황이나, 수주 회복과 5 공장 매출 인식 본격화, 미국 공장 및 멀티 모
달리티 확장 등 중장기 성장 방향성에 주목할 필요가 있다.

COMPLIANCE NOTICE 
작성자(이선경)는 본 조사분석자료에 게재된 내용들이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭없이 신의성실하게 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
본 보고서에 언급된 종목의 경우 당사 조사분석담당자는 본인의 담당종목을 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
본 보고서는 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공된 사실이 없습니다. 
당사는 자료공표일 현재 해당기업과 관련하여 특별한 이해 관계가 없습니다. 
종목별 투자의견은 다음과 같습니다. 
투자판단 3 단계(6 개월기준) 15%이상 → 매수 / -15%~15% → 중립 / -15%미만 → 매도

3Q26Re: 신규 시설 매출 인식 개시 
연결 기준 매출액 1조 4,903억원(+18.5%YoY, 이하 YoY 생략), 영업이익 6,401억원
(+1.0%, OPM 42.9%)로 컨센서스를 상회하는 실적 기록. 1~4공장이 완전 가동을 유지하는 
한편, 5공장은 PPQ 배치 생산 본격화되면서 매출 기여 확대. 상업 생산 전환이 예상되는 
2H27부터 이익 기여 커질 것으로 기대. 
연초 인수한 Rockville 공장은 3Q에도 안정적인 상업 생산을 이어갔으며 2Q 생산 물량의 
매출이 인식되며 매출 성장에 기여. 동사는 Rockville 공장 매출 인식 등을 반영하여 기존 
+15~20% 성장에서 +20%로 연간 가이던스 상향.   
고객사 수주 회복 기대 
3Q26 기준 누적 수주액은 219억 달러로 전분기 대비 소폭 증가했으나 과거 연간 수주액 
증가 대비 부족한 수준. 저조한 수주 실적은 지난해 초 의약품에 대한 관세 불확실성 증가로 
고객사들의 수주 문의가 저조했던 영향. 26년 초부터 관세 불확실성이 낮아지며 수주 문의
는 회복 추세. 약 1년 정도 논의 기간이 소요되는 것을 고려할 때 26년말~27년 초 수주 회
복 확인 가능할 것으로 기대. 
안정적인 공급망 유지가 중요한 CDMO 산업 특성상 노조 파업 가능성도 고객사 수주를 늦
추는 요인으로 판단. 다만, 10/16 인천 고등법원이 쟁의행위 가처분 항고심에 대해 인용 결
정을 하며 기존 3개 공정에서 9개 공정으로 확대되며 파업 우려는 다소 완화. 
투자 의견 Buy, 목표 주가 200만원 유지 
매수 의견과 목표 주가 200만원을 유지. 원달러 약세에도 불구하고 5공장 Ramp-up과 
Rockville 공장 매출 기여로 견조한 실적 성장 지속될 것으로 예상. 파업 이슈 해소와 대형 
수주 계약이 주가 상승을 이끌 것으로 판단.

Document 267: AI is boosting business creations both in the US and Europe (2026-11-04)

4 November 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
AI IS BOOSTING BUSINESS CREATIONS BOTH IN THE US AND EUROPE
Stéphane Colliac
The significant increase in business creations since mid-2025 in the United States, France and Germany – and, to a lesser extent, in the United
Kingdom – is continuing. Across the board, this trend is being driven by the information and communication sector and mostly by AI. It is
spreading to other business services, while developments in consumer services, industry and construction are more mixed. However, in both

the United States and France, the trend is broadening, suggesting that AI is facilitating business creation across all sectors.
Business creations by sector: a boom that is building momentum and spreading

We have calculated the year-on-year change in the number of new business start-ups. The table categorises these results as positive (red), neutral (white) or negative (blue). The darker
the colour, the larger the change. The data series are defined as follows: Germany = new entries in the companies register (start-ups) since 2008; France = business start-ups, including

micro-entrepreneurs, since 2000; United Kingdom = businesses registered for VAT/PAYE since 2017; United States = business applications (tax identification number) since 2004. Sectors
have been grouped together to ensure that the classification is standardised across all countries. For the third quarter, the German data only include the months of July and August.

### Page 2

2
The bank for
a changing
world
Chart of the Week / 4 November 2026 economic-research.bnpparibas.com
A noticeable boom in the United States, France, and Germany, but not quite as pronounced in the United Kingdom
The four economies for which we have comparable data (the United States, France, Germany and the United Kingdom) have all
seen, to varying degrees, an increase in the number of new business creations. While the timing varies across these countries,

most of them are enjoying strong momentum. In the United States, there has been a noticeable acceleration since the second
quarter of 2025, which has continued to strengthen. Germany has followed a similar trajectory, moving from slight declines at
the start of 2024 to sharp increases. France is experiencing the same trend but with some specific features, as momentum has
picked up significantly after months of slowdown (due to the uncertainty following the dissolution of the National Assembly) and is

accelerating across most sectors in 2026. In the United Kingdom, the trend is less pronounced and the increase may not last.
Business services, and AI in particular, are driving this momentum
In most of these four countries, business creations are rising significantly in the information and communication sector, which is
directly linked to AI. In the United States, Germany and France, there has been a pronounced trend which is gaining momentum.
In the United Kingdom, the AI sector is also seeing an increase, but it is significantly less pronounced. However, this momentum is

not limited to this sector. It is spreading to many services sectors, including finance, real estate and business services. Growth has
been sustained since mid-2025 in the United States and Germany, and since the third quarter of 2025 in France.
The picture for consumer services is more mixed. In the United States, trade, accommodation and catering have experienced major
increases in creations since mid-2025. In France, these sectors are in positive territory, particularly trade, where the increase in

business creations has recently accelerated. In Germany, these same sectors fell into negative territory in 2026 following a rather
mixed year in 2025.
The situation is even more mixed for manufacturing and construction. In the United States, these sectors are also part of the
overall positive momentum in the third quarter of 2026. This shows that AI does not only benefit sectors closely linked to it
(notably the digital technology sector) but that it makes it easier to start a business across the entire economy (by simplifying

administrative procedures in particular). In France, the upturn in business creations in the construction sector suggests that this
same trend is at play, even as activity in the sector continues to contract.
Therefore, the trend is more widespread in the United States and is becoming increasingly prevalent in France. It is driven by
specific sectors of the services industry in Germany, while it is significantly more subdued in the United Kingdom.

stephane.colliac@bnpparibas.com

Document 314: Could Midterms Give Wall Street A Cold Shower_

Source PDF: all report pdf/Could Midterms Give Wall Street A Cold Shower_.pdf
Report source: researchmail轉寄
Date: 2026-11-03

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: Could Midterms Give Wall Street A Cold Shower?
Date:    1 hour ago
Could Midterms Give Wall Street A Cold Shower?

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
Could Midterms Give Wall Street
A Cold Shower?
By Ed Yardeni & Elias Griepentrog • 2 Nov 2026
View in browser

I. When Gridlock Isn't Good for Stocks
The US midterm elections will be over tomorrow night. According to
betting markets, Democrats have roughly a 95% probability of winning the
House and a 76% probability of taking the Senate (charts).
We suspect that stock investors have yet to fully discount the possibility of
a Democratic sweep of Congress. Some may remain skeptical that polls
confirming strong support for Democrats are accurate. They might reckon

### Page 2

that Democrats are more likely than Republicans to talk to pollsters over
the phone.
In any event, Wall Street traditionally welcomes political gridlock because
it reduces the likelihood that Congress will pass extremist legislation. But
this time could be different for two reasons.
First, deepening partisanship could turn gridlock into damaging political
paralysis at a time when the Bond Vigilantes are already uneasy about
Washington's unsustainable fiscal trajectory. A Democratic sweep could

complicate negotiations over government spending and the debt ceiling.
Prolonged shutdowns and fiscal standoffs could push Treasury yields
higher, weighing on equity valuations.

### Page 3

Second, gridlock's traditional ability to constrain policymaking may not
hold this time. President Donald Trump has relied heavily on executive
authority, with some of his most market-disruptive policies, including tariffs
and military action against Iran, pursued largely without congressional
approval. A Democratic-controlled Congress may therefore do little to
restrain him. Moreover, with the midterms behind him, Trump may feel
even less constrained politically and more willing to pursue controversial

policies through executive action. Hence, a blue wave could deliver a cold
shower for stocks, though we would view a significant selloff as a buying
opportunity given the remarkable strength of the economy and corporate
earnings.
One encouraging development ahead of the elections was today's reports
that the US and Iran are working on a peaceful diplomatic solution to their
war. Iranian President Masoud Pezeshkian said he has granted his
government greater authority to pursue peace talks, while President

Trump and Vice President Vance also signaled progress. Hopes for a deal
sent the price of Brent crude oil down 7.6% to $104.77 per barrel (chart).
However, we have been here before with apparent diplomatic
breakthroughs. We still see a high risk of military re-escalation. The bid-
ask spread remains wide, with Tehran demanding sanctions relief, the
release of frozen assets, and an end to the US naval blockade of Iranian
ports, while Washington insists on nuclear concessions. Meanwhile, Iran's

leverage over the Strait of Hormuz has been weakening. According to
shipping data, for roughly half of September, Gulf oil exporters shipped
more crude than they were shipping before the war.

### Page 4

II. US Economy Continues To Roar
The US economy remains strong despite the volatility of the domestic
political and geopolitical scenes. Consider the following:
(1) Construction spending. Construction spending fell 1.9% m/m in
September to $2.19 trillion (saar), reversing most of August's upwardly
revised 2.0% increase. Nevertheless, spending remains just below last
year's record high (chart).
September's decline was driven by a 5.5% m/m drop in private residential

construction, attributable to a 13.0% plunge in home improvements
(chart). Elevated materials costs and higher mortgage rates continue to
weigh on the housing sector. Meanwhile, private nonresidential
construction rose 1.1% m/m, supported by continued investment in data
centers and power infrastructure.

### Page 5

The AI investment boom remains a powerful tailwind. Data center
construction spending surged 8.6% m/m and 90% y/y in September,
reaching a record $96.5 billion annual rate (chart).
(2) Manufacturing. The ISM M-PMI rose from 54.5 in September to 55.5
in October, marking its tenth consecutive month of expansion, the longest
streak since 2022. Production jumped to the highest reading since
November 2021, while new orders edged up to 55.5. Employment also

remained in expansionary territory (chart).

### Page 6

Inflationary pressures remain troublesome. The prices-paid index eased
from 77.9 to 75.2 in October but remained above 70 for the ninth
consecutive month. Regional Fed surveys also point to persistent price
pressures. Higher energy costs, tariffs, and elevated metals prices
continue to boost input costs (chart).
(3) Bank lending. The Fed's latest Senior Loan Officer Opinion Survey
(SLOOS) painted a mixed picture of credit demand. Banks reported
weaker demand for commercial and industrial (C&I) loans, mortgages,

and auto loans in Q4, while credit-card demand strengthened (chart).

### Page 7

Lending standards remained broadly unchanged for C&I loans but
tightened somewhat for commercial real estate loans (chart).
Encouragingly, actual bank lending tells a happier story. C&I loans surged
11.3% y/y through October 21, while total loans and leases increased
7.9% y/y (chart).

### Page 8

Credit growth is broad across bank sizes, with lending up 8.1% y/y at
large banks and 5.4% at small banks (chart).
Meanwhile, banks' allowances for loan and lease losses have remained
relatively stable at around $202 billion, despite the acceleration in lending
(chart).

### Page 9

clause.

Economics | 3

### Page 4

Monthly Economics
Sector Analysis
Personal Consumption Expenditures (PCE)
• We've lifted our Q3 expectation for real PCE on incoming data and a slightly firmer consumer
backdrop in the wake of recent revisions.
Consumer spending looks firmer in the third quarter, and we now expect real PCE to rise at a 3.4%
annualized clip. This upward revision reflects recent data, with August consumption coming in firm
with real spending rising by the fastest monthly clip in nearly two years, but also reflects a slightly

firmer consumer backdrop in the wake of the BEA's annual revisions released late September. Although
revisions did not materially alter spending, they lifted the level of household income in recent years,
which also boosted the personal saving rate. The trajectory of real income growth remains weak, but
the consumer still looks to be in a slightly stronger financial position than previously believed, which
helps explain recent resilience in spending and suggests consumers may have a bit more runway ahead.

Investment: Equipment, Intellectual Property Products and Inventories
• The AI build out will lead to another surge in business fixed investment spending in Q3, though the
effect on headline growth will be partly offset by stronger imports.
Business fixed investment spending continues to be supported primarily by the high-tech buildout.
We forecast real equipment investment to rise at around a 16% annualized clip in Q3, but part of
this boost to headline GDP growth will be offset by stronger import growth that is facilitating the

build out. Net imports of computers are tracking to surge in the third quarter, which will provide a
sizable lift to information processing equipment investment, or around half of overall equipment.
And while non-defense capital goods shipments (including aircraft) slipped in August, the near-term
growth trajectory remains supportive of a pickup in equipment. The rebuild in inventories we have
been anticipating is also tracking to be a bit stronger as businesses replenish drawn down product.

Investment: Residential
• The abrupt rise in mortgage rates is likely to dampen residential investment over the next few
quarters. Residential spending should find support next year as longer-term interest rates
moderate somewhat.
We are unlikely to see another positive residential investment reading in Q3. Rising mortgage rates

have sent a chill over builder outlooks, pushing sales expectations to their weakest level in more than
three years. While builders have responded with deeper price cuts, affordability pressures continue
to weigh on single-family sales and construction outlays. Apartment construction is simultaneously
soft amid rising financing costs and modest rent growth, and home improvement spending remains
constrained by high materials costs and interest rates. While residential investment is likely to remain

4 | Economics

### Page 5

Economic Outlook: October 2026 Economics
a drag this year, we expect a slight moderation in longer-term interest rates in 2027, which would
provide a more supportive backdrop.
Investment: Nonresidential Structures
• We have not made material changes to our nonresidential structures forecast. Despite elevated
interest rates, data center and related spending should continue to support structures investment
through our forecast horizon.
Nonresidential structures spending has accelerated sharply, overwhelmingly driven by data centers

and electric power facilities. As data center construction expands geographically and demand rises
for semiconductors, high-tech capex has also started to generate positive spillovers for warehousing
and manufacturing construction. This buildout, which appears relatively insensitive to interest rates,
should be enough to sustain solid growth in structures investment even as spending across most
other categories remains essentially flat. Domestic energy is another green shoot, with elevated prices

supporting ongoing growth in active rotary rigs.

Net Exports & Trade
• An expected drag from trade in Q3 got larger in light of a near-record trade deficit in August, net
exports is now expected to lop off three full percentage points from third quarter growth.

Exports increased in August for the first time in four months, but the increase was more than offset by
an even larger increase in imports. The upshot was a widening in the trade deficit to a near-record gap
of more than $105 billion. Going into the report, we had a 1.9 percentage point drag from net exports
penciled in for Q3 GDP; we revised that to a full 3.0 percentage points drag in light of the record gap.
Even a modest narrowing in the final months of the year would likely mean a lift to headline GDP in

Q4, but the real story here is that trade will remain a headwind throughout the forecast period owing
largely to surging imports of tech-related products amid the AI boom.
Labor Market
• The jobs market remains stable. We continue to expect the unemployment rate to end the year at
4.2% and hold near that level in 2027.
We modestly revised down our near-term payroll growth forecast following the September jobs
report, but otherwise we have left our labor market outlook unchanged. Measures of labor demand

growth continue to point to a stable labor market. Job openings have inched higher on a year-ago
basis but remain broadly steady while initial jobless claims, WARN notices and Challenger job cut
announcements continue to signal low layoffs. Labor force participation appears to be improving after
its sharp pullback in the first half of the year. Looking ahead, we expect payroll growth to average
around 60K per month in 2027, roughly in line with our estimate of breakeven job growth. That should

Economics | 5

### Page 6

Monthly Economics
keep the unemployment rate near 4.2% in 2027 and leave labor market conditions broadly consistent
with "full employment." Notably, our expectation for labor cost growth of ~3.4% is the reason we
continue to reiterate that labor cost growth is not a contributor to the excess inflation seen in the
data.
Inflation
• We expect the core PCE deflator to rise 3.2% and 2.6% Q4/Q4 in 2026 and 2027, respectively.
Headline inflation remains heavily influenced by developments in the Middle East, with higher oil

prices lifting gasoline and diesel prices. Food inflation has been tame this year but looks poised to
strengthen as higher transportation and commodity prices work their way through supply chains.
Beyond food and energy, price pressures remain concentrated in a handful of categories, most notably
airfares and AI-related tech goods. Core goods inflation is showing signs of stabilizing, as waning tariff-
related price pressures have been offset by resilient demand tied to the AI buildout. We expect some

improvement here in 2027. Still-low shelter inflation and cooler price growth for airfares and health
care should help bring about some moderation in core services inflation next year. We expect the core
PCE deflator to rise 3.2% on a Q4-over-Q4 basis in 2026 before slowing to 2.6% in 2027.

Fiscal Policy
• Fiscal year (FY) 2026 ended on September 30, and the federal government tallied a $2.0 trillion
budget deficit (6.5% of GDP). We project the deficit to be unchanged in dollar terms in FY 2027

and a bit smaller as a share of GDP (~6%).
• Another continuing resolution to extend government funding past the December 11 deadline
seems like the most likely outcome at this time for the FY 2027 budget. It is possible Republicans
use the lame duck session of Congress to increase defense spending and raise the debt ceiling.
Looking ahead to the lame duck session of Congress that will follow the midterm elections, Congress
needs to pass a bill that extends government funding past December 11 to avert a government

shutdown. Another continuing resolution that punts the issue into Q1 seems like the most likely
outcome. Perhaps more interesting will be whether Republicans attempt to use budget reconciliation
to pass one more partisan piece of legislation. If they do, we suspect the two things at the top the
agenda will be more funding for national defense and immigration enforcement priorities as well as a
debt ceiling increase. For the latter, raising it in the lame duck would remove some leverage Democrats

could have in 2027 if they recapture one or both chambers. It is still very early, but we estimate the
debt ceiling "X date" by which Congress must act or face default is sometime in late Q3/early Q4 of
next year.
6 | Economics

### Page 7

Economic Outlook: October 2026 Economics
Monetary Policy & Interest Rates
• Our base case forecast is for the FOMC to hike the fed funds rate by 25 bps in December and
then keep the policy rate unchanged in 2027 at 4.00%-4.25%, with risks skewed toward additional
tightening.
As we go to print, the FOMC seems more likely than not to keep the fed funds rate unchanged in
October. But given the rationale under which the Fed started this tightening cycle and the forcefulness

with which Warsh has driven home his displeasure with elevated inflation, it seems entirely plausible
to us that October will be a live meeting. At a minimum, the recent inflation data seem to make a hike
in December a lock. Past December, the outlook will be highly dependent on future inflation data. Our
expectation is that core inflation will cool gradually in 2027, and we still do not see signs of demand-
side inflation in the data. But hiking cycles have an inertial component to them, and the recent inflation

data reset the clock to zero on the Fed seeing several good inflation prints in a row. This creates upside
risk to our base case for the fed funds rate.

International Developments
• Global growth remains resilient, leaving our 2026 and 2027 forecasts unchanged. Our inflation
forecasts are also unchanged, although risks of delayed price pass-through could prolong inflation
pressures into 2027.

• Monetary policy continues to lean toward tightening across advanced economies, while emerging
market central banks are increasingly cautious, either holding rates or easing more gradually as
inflation pressures rebuild.
The balance of risks to global inflation remains tilted to the upside, complicating the monetary policy
outlook even as growth has proven more resilient than expected. Elevated energy prices, particularly
for refined petroleum products, persistent geopolitical uncertainty and El Niño-related food price

risks could prolong inflationary pressures into 2027. While second-round effects still remain relatively
contained, repeated supply shocks are testing central banks’ willingness to look through the temporary
price increases, particularly where inflation expectations remain elevated, even as higher government
bond yields are already contributing to tighter financial conditions.
Across advanced economies, central banks face increasingly difficult trade-offs as persistent inflation

pressures limit the scope for policy accommodation. The Eurozone remains particularly vulnerable
to energy supply disruptions, with elevated natural gas prices and low storage levels adding to
inflation risks. We continue to expect a 25 bps European Central Bank hike in December, followed by a
prolonged hold through 2027. Fiscal concerns are also adding to pressure, particularly in France, where
the selloff in government debt has pushed yields higher. In the U.K., resilient activity and renewed

Economics | 7

### Page 8

Monthly Economics
inflation pressures similarly underpin our expectation for a Bank of England hike in November,
followed by a hold through 2027.
Elsewhere, policy paths are becoming more differentiated. After four hikes this year brought the Cash
Rate to 4.60%, we expect the Reserve Bank of Australia to remain on hold, although renewed inflation
pressures or stronger-than-expected activity could prompt further tightening. We also still expect
the Bank of Canada to hold at 2.25%, with subdued growth weighing against energy-driven inflation

risks, although incoming inflation data over the coming weeks will be critical in determining whether
persistent price pressures warrant tightening monetary policy perhaps as early as the December
meeting. Japan remains an exception to the broader hold outlook: while the yen's recovery provides
some relief from imported inflation, elevated energy prices and import dependence continue to pose
upside risks. We expect the Bank of Japan to hike again by year-end and now expect an additional 25

bps hike in Q3-2027, bringing the terminal rate to 1.75%.
The emerging market story has increasingly shifted from easing toward patience or selective
tightening. In Asia, China's weak domestic demand and persistent property-sector strains continue to
warrant policy support, with AI-related exports providing some offset. We expect a more gradual pace
of Reserve Requirement Ratio cuts alongside targeted monetary and fiscal measures. India faces the

opposite challenge, with elevated food and energy prices and rupee depreciation strengthening the
case for further Reserve Bank of India tightening. In Latin America, peso depreciation and elevated
energy costs pose upside inflation risks for Mexico, although limited exchange-rate pass-through
should allow Banxico to remain on hold while policymakers monitor underlying inflation and inflation
expectations. In Brazil, election-related uncertainty and renewed inflation pressures are likely to delay

further easing, with cuts expected to resume in Q1-2027 once policymakers see more convincing
evidence of disinflation.

### Page 1

Muted iPhone 18 Pro Cycle and Rising Memory Costs

[Table_Rank]

Maintained  Hold  
 Target price US$334

[Table_Summary] Lower TP to $334, Hold rating: Driven by investors being away from AI themes, Apple share 
price outperformed Nasdaq by 7.2% in the past one month. However, we continue to expect 
muted cycle of iPhone 18 P/PM with constrained volume of iPhone Duo. Additionally, memory 
cost hike will last longer than expected, driven by our view of tight DRAM/NAND supply, and 
we expect cost hikes throughout CY2027. For hardware, we still expect the iPhone 20 (2H27) 
to be a strong product cycle, thanks to new design of display, followed by Apple’s early move 
to TSMC ’s A14 in 2H28 (suggesting only 2 -years at 2nm). Additionally, Apple Intelligence 
remains the key to watch. Yet, the share price trades at 35x FY27E P/E, suggesting the stock 
isn’t cheap. By factoring in results, guidance and lower shipments/margins, we revise FY27E 
EPS by -10% and revise TP from $369 to $334, still based on 35x FY27E. 
Dec-Q margin guidance below expect ations: Apple delivered solid Sep t-Q results, with 
revenue of $114.5bn (+12% YoY) and diluted EPS of $2.1 (+14% YoY), both ahead of 
consensus. For Dec-Q, mgmt. guided to total revenue growth of 7 –10% YoY (vs. consensus 
of +6.5%), Services growth of 8 –9%, and gross margin of 45 –46%. The revenue outlook is 
constructive to Street expectations, though the margin guidance and Services deceleration 
were disappointing, reflecting cost hikes and mix headwinds.  
The iPhone product cycle mixed, with challenges in FY2027: Mgmt sees a strong start of 
iPhone Duo but remains supply constrained. Hence, we continue to expect the EMS build plan 
of 6.5m of iPhone Duo by end-2026 with limited upside, while the mainstream iPhone 18 P/PM 
to be 74.5m. As said in our earlier report of iPhone 18 Pro/Pro Max Lukewarm on Sept 15, we 
believe the muted demand will weigh on CY1H27 EMS builds. Margin wise, despite p ricing 
actions taken earlier, margin will likely be under pressure, as the CEO Ternus characterized 
current memory pricing as “unprecedented,” with higher costs in December and beyond.  
Apple positioning the rebuilt Siri AI as a core differentiator: The CEO Ternus emphasized 
that the new Siri is deeply integrated and privacy -preserving and is fielding ~2.5bn requests 
per day. From a financial perspective, CFO Parekh noted that these Si ri AI costs are 
embedded in the December margin outlook, suggesting near-term gross-margin compression 
alongside the memory cost up-cycle.  
Risks: 1) weak demand, 2) competition, 3) product delays, 4) geo-tension.

### Page 2

Earnings Forecast  
Solid September Quarter Results  
Apple delivered a solid September-quarter, with revenue of $114.5bn (+12% YoY) and diluted EPS of 
$2.10 (+14% YoY), both ahead of consensus, reaching a record fiscal year of $479bn in revenue. Gross 
margin came in at 47.9%, aided by approximately one percentage point of tariff refund benefits (and a 
$0.05 EPS tailwind).  
iPhone revenue reached a September-quarter record of $57.6bn (+17.5% YoY) likely driven by higher 
ASPs, Mac rose a strong 24% to $10.8bn likely on edge-AI, Services set at $31.6bn (+9.8% YoY) which 
was below expectations, while iPad declined 10% to $6.2bn and Wearables fell 8% to $8.3bn. The 
company returned $28bn to shareholders. 
December Quarter Guidance Slightly Ahead in Revenue but Shy in Margin  
For the December quarter, mgmt. guided to total revenue growth of 7 –10% YoY ( vs. consensus of 
+6.5%), Services growth of 8–9%, and gross margin of 45–46%. Operating expenses are expected at 
$20.4–20.8bn, OI&E around $300 million, and the tax rate near 17%. The revenue outlook is 
constructive relative to prior Street expectations, though the margin range and Services deceleration 
reflect known cost and mix headwinds.

### Page 5

Valuation and Recommendation  
Hold with TP of $334 
We rate Apple (AAPL US) Hold and set our TP at $334 which is based on 35x our FY27E EPS estimate. 
We believe it is important to capture the 2H26 -3Q27’s iPhone 18 product cycle, given high market 
interest. We adopt P/E methodology as we believe the market now mainly focuses on the company’s 
earnings outlook, and we usually apply P/E method to downstream firms.  
We believe Apple’s ecosystem will remain strong and associated revenue durability is high. This is 
backed by Apple's installed base growth, double digit YoY growth in services. However, the market 
would wait for visibility for its Apple Intelligence. Meanwhile, the market will need more visibility on the 
higher-high retail prices for the iPhone 18 series due to the rising cost over tariff and 2nm/memories. 
That said, we expect its earnings growth to be  high-teens to 20% YoY in FY26E-FY28E, from single 
digit YoY in FY22-FY24. Considering >30x one-year P/E, the stock doesn’t look particularly cheap.  
Figure 4: Apple’s 12-month forward P/E

Sources: Bloomberg, GF Securities (Hong Kong) Brokerage

1) weak demand, 2) competition, 3) product delays, 4) geo-tension.

► 近一週美股 、日股及新興市場表現遜於港股
及歐股 ，因市場擔憂民主黨全面掌控國會恐
影響AI基礎建設擴張進程 ，令股市承壓 ，即
便總統川普表態接受共和黨敗選結果並願尋
求民 主 黨 的跨 黨 合 作，仍 無 法緩 解 市 場擔
憂，美股主要指數普遍收跌；歐股方面 ，面
對全球科技股評價修正 ，部分資金轉入歐洲

市場尋求避險 ，使歐股近期呈現跌深反彈；
亞股方面 ，日股因整體 AI供應鏈與美國高度
連結而同步下跌 ，港股則是受惠騰訊發布新
AI模型帶動相關版塊大漲。
► 市場擔憂美股科技巨頭財報不如預期 、AI基
礎建設受限及資本開支見頂 、負自由現金流
量等因素 ，導致市場資金從半導體 、資訊科
技與 通 訊 服務 股 大 幅流 出，並轉 進 必 需消
費、醫療保健、公用事業等防禦性產業。

熱門議題
► 黃金價格歷經第二季修整 ，近日呈現大幅反
彈走勢 ，主要係因美國期中選舉後 ，市場憂
心AI產業監管將趨嚴 ，加上晶片與科技股遭
遇大幅修正 ，促使部分資金轉往黃金等防禦
型資產尋求避險。
► 根據世界黃金協會最新報告 ，第3季全球央行
淨買進321公噸黃金，較第二季明顯回升，年
增率達42%，創下自2024年第四季以來的單
季新高，前3季累計買進金額更是高達 675公
噸，顯示黃金儲備已經列入全球央行必需資
產配置而且比重維持穩定增長態勢。
► 長期以來黃金具有抗通膨特性 ，觀察黃金與
聯邦基金利率走勢 ，從2020年之後 ，即便全
球歷經2次升息循環，黃金價格仍呈現穩健上
揚走勢 ，反應其抗通膨保值特性 。此外，美
國財政赤字壓力難消 ，恐令美元長期購買力
被稀釋 ， 加 速 全 球 央 行 去 美 元 化， 有 鑑 於
此，建議黃金納入資產配置。
黃金走勢vs美元指數vs聯邦基金利率全球央行最新黃金儲備量

內部參考文件請勿外流
資料來源：Bloomberg，Factset，凱基證券整理；註：右上產業回報相關性為各產業過去3年之每週報酬相關性
► 醫療類股於美國第三季財報展現強勁的獲利
韌性，實際獲利優於預期程度為主要產業中
表現最佳，背後主要受惠大型製藥企業 (如：
禮來、再生元製藥、吉利德科學)在減重藥、
癌症治療及創新藥領域之需求持續增長 ，帶
動整體產業獲利表現優於市場預期。
► 期中選舉後醫療政策輪廓逐漸明朗 。民主黨
主張支持擴大醫療保險覆蓋 、延續平價醫療
法案補貼以及緩和醫療補助計劃 (Medicaid)
削減，有助提升醫療服務使用率；雖藥價談
判政策恐帶來部分定價壓力 ，但整體政策主
張仍對創新藥 、生技研發及醫療保險產業具
潛在政策支持效果。
► 綜上，在AI投資回報率面臨市場檢驗之際 ，
市場資金持續尋求防禦性配置 。醫療保健類
股與資訊科技類股相關性偏低 ，有助分散投
資組合對單一 AI題材依賴 ，加上第三季獲利
動能轉強 、創新藥需求穩健及產業能見度提
升，後續有望持續受惠資金輪動效應。

財報與政策題材雙利多，醫療類股成市場新寵兒

政策議題 民主黨傾向 共和黨傾向 兩黨共識對製藥產業潛在影響
降低藥價 依《通膨削減法案》框架，擴大醫療 補助計劃(Medicaid)藥價談判適用範圍 傾向透過最惠國定價機制來降低藥 價 兩黨均主張降低藥價 製藥企業面臨定價壓力
美國本土製造 透過研發補助、稅收優惠及產業政策 鼓勵藥廠回流美國生產 透過關稅、供應鏈重組及企業承諾 推動製藥產能回流 兩黨均支持醫藥供應鏈自主化 有利本土藥廠及原料藥供應鏈投資
醫療保險覆蓋
支持延長《平價醫療法案》補貼、擴
大醫療補助計劃(Medicaid)覆蓋範圍，
並緩和該補助計劃的削減
傾向由州政府及市場機制主導醫療
體系，限制聯邦政府支出
醫療的可負擔性為兩黨政策共同焦點，有助
增加就醫人口及處方藥使用量，支撐製藥、
醫療服務及保險業長期需求
FDA審查資源 支持增加FDA審查預算與人力配置 著重監管效率優化，額外資源投入 相對有限 兩黨均支持提升新藥審查效率 利多創新藥與生技公司

投資焦點必需消費
► 短期而言 ，檢視各類股在最近股災表現 ，美
國成長股 、資訊科技 、與通訊服務等高波動
度類股 ，表現明顯落後大盤 ，其中，資訊科
技與通訊服務波動度最大 、跌幅最重 ，主要
係因美股科技巨頭財報不如預期 、負自由現
金流量 ，疊加期中選舉後民主黨恐緊縮 AI基
礎建設進程及資本支出見頂等不確定因素 ，
令市場 擔憂 導致 資 金流出 ；並轉 進核 心消
費、健康護理 、防禦股 、公用事業等防禦性
產業，報酬相對較穩健，受市場資金青睞。
► 長期而言，從2008迄今，核心消費類股長期
表現穩健上漲 。與主要指數相比 ，面對近月
AI科技股修正 ，同期間費半大跌 18%、那斯
達克跌幅 6%、標普跌幅 1%，而核心消費類
股大漲13%，反應其低波動、抗震韌性。
► 整體而言 ，從2015年至今 ，歷經2次通膨與
升息循環 ，核心消費受影響性低 ，有鑑於資
產配置角度 ，建議短期跟隨資金流向配置核
心消費股，提高投組抗震韌性。
必需消費長期表現穩健、抗通膨、低波動避險資金流入，必需消費類股單月表現最佳

配置必需消費類股，提高投組抗震韌性

股市
 隨著美國期中選舉結束 ，美伊衝突可望降溫 ，市場關注焦點將回歸基本面 ，惟AI資料中心建設進程
或將受到州政府緊縮政策而造成評價面壓力增添股價波動 。短期可布局防禦股 、分散配置於不同國
家與產業，增強抗震力、降低市場波動影響 ，長期而言，AI從全面看好變成要追求財務健全度較高
的品質股、且大優於小，震盪之後長期正向趨勢不變。
 歐、日股可分散配置 ，利率走升有利銀行股 。此外，隨財政政策支持 ，歐洲國防軍工仍有投資題材 。
策略風格：短期可增加防禦股配置，AI從
全面看好變成要追求財務健全度較高的品
質股、且大優於小，震盪之後長期正向趨
勢不變，非AI部分可關注必需消費產業、
醫療保健產業
區域：日本銀行股、歐洲國防
債市
 隨資金湧入 ，公債殖利率回落 ，惟根據最新聯準會利率點陣圖顯示 ，官員預估今年底前仍有一碼升
息空間，立場偏向維持較高利率水平抑制通膨發展 。建議以中短天期債券投資為主 ，可逢利率走升
時布局進行鎖利領息 。高信評債體質較佳 ，具穩定現金流與還款能力 ，聚焦風險調整後利差具吸引

力者，包含金融、通訊及公用事業等產業。
債種：中短天期高信評債鎖利，產業如金
融、通訊及公用事業等
匯市
 聯準會12月份會議升息態度偏鷹，美元高檔整理。
 日本銀行9月升息，但前瞻指引偏中性，未如市場預期偏鷹，日圓弱勢盤整。
美元高檔整理，日圓弱勢盤整
商品
 美國期中選舉結束，預期中東地緣衝突降溫，油價從近期高點回落，短線仍偏震盪。
 去美元化支撐全球央行買盤，提供黃金中長期穩定支撐。
低接買盤流入，黃金低點反彈，中長期維
持偏多看法

個別客戶因年齡、投資能力、金融商品交易經驗及風險承受度不同，適合之服務及/或金融商品亦有不同，應進一步與專屬理財業務人員進行評估。投資人應徹底瞭解相關服務及金融商品之風險 ，並審慎評估個人財力、資產狀況、
理財需求及風險承受能力，獨立判斷並自行決定是否投資及承擔有關風險。投資任何商品皆具有風險，商品以往之績效不保證未來之投資收益，本公司不負責商品之盈虧，也不保證最低之收益。

等行為究責。
基金警語
投資一定有風險，基金投資有賺有賠，申購前應詳閱公開說明書
【信託產品投資須知及風險預告：（請投資人審慎評估下列各項條文內容）】
1. 基金（包括具有定期配息之基金）投資具投資風險，此一風險可能使信託本金發生全部虧損。
2. 基金委託人須自負盈虧，本公司受託投資不保本不保息，除盡善良管理人注意義務外，投資所可能產生的本金虧損、匯率損失、或基金解散、清算、移轉、合併等風險，均由委託人承擔。

3. 本公司受託投資之基金經金管會核准或同意生效，惟不表示絕無風險。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良管理人之注意義務外，不負責基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，委託人
申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 投資前應詳閱風險預告及各該基金公開說明書 ，確實作好財務規劃與風險評估 。本公司受託投資之基金已備有公開說明書 (或其中譯本)或投資人須知【前述文件併揭露有關基金應負擔之費用 (境外基金含分銷費用)】，委託人可
至本公司營業處所索取或至公開資訊觀測站、基金資訊觀測站或本公司網站下載。
5. 為保護既有投資人之權益，部份境外基金設有價格稀釋調整機制及公平市價規定，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書及投資人須知。
6. 委託人瞭解並同意受託人辦理特定金錢信託業務(即委託人委託投資基金)交易時，自交易相對人取得之報酬、費用、折讓等各項利益，得作為受託人收取之信託報酬；另委託人在持有基金期間 ，受託人仍持續收受經理費分成報
酬，委託人可至受託人(即本公司)網站 (https://www.kgi.com.tw/zh-tw/)查詢基金之通路報酬。其基金經理費率及其分成費率與其他通路報酬最新變動情形，亦可至該網站查詢。

7. 後收型基金在贖回時，基金公司將依持有期間長短收取不同比率之遞延申購手續費 ，該費用將自贖回總額中扣除；另後收型基金若有收取分銷費 ，則申購手續費雖可遞延收取，惟每年仍需支付0%~1.06%的分銷費（按基金日平
均淨資產價值計算），可能造成實際負擔費用增加（分銷費依各基金公司而有不同，請參考本公司後收型基金投資費用暨特約事項及各基金之投資人須知），另該費用已反映於每日基金淨值中，委託人無需額外支付。
8. 委託人之交易如經基金公司認定為短線交易者，基金公司或本公司可拒絕受理申請轉換或申購要求，並可請求支付一定比例之贖回費用或其他相關費用，費用標準均依各該基金公司之規定。
9. 委託人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績效亦不代表未來績效之保證。基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動，投資人於獲配息時，宜一併注意基
金淨值之變動。又投資新興市場可能比投資已開發國家有較大的價格波動及流動性較低的風險，委託人應慎選投資標的。
10. 部分基金可能持有衍生性商品部位，該部位可能超過基金淨資產價值之100%，並可能因而造成基金淨值高度波動及衍生其他風險。

11. 基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部分，可能導致原始投資金額減損；且部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之費用。由本金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至證券投資信託事業或總代理人之公司
網站查詢。
12. 非投資等級債券基金（本類基金主要係投資於非投資等級之高風險債券且配息來源可能為本金）（主要特色係投資前揭基金之組合基金亦同），適合能承受較高風險之『非保守型』委託人且不宜占投資組合過高的比重。由於非
投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損，不適合無法承擔相關風險之委託人；又如
係本國投信機構募集者，則可能投資於符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，雖其投資總金額不得超過基金淨資產價值之百分之三十，然該債券具有債券發行人違約之信用風險、利率風險、流動性風險、交易對手風險
及因財務訊息揭露不完整或因價格不透明導致波動性較大之風險，投資人應審慎評估；另證券投資信託事業募集之債券型基金、平衡型基金、不動產證券化基金、以追蹤、模擬或複製標的指數表現之指數股票型基金（ETF）及

指數型基金或募集之基金投資新興市場國家債券達基金淨資產價值之百分之六十以上者，或有可能投資符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，亦應留意相關風險。
13. 投資於中國之基金應遵守中華民國相關法令之規定：境外基金 (不含經金管會核准向證交所申請上市或交易之境外 ETF)如有投資大陸地區之有價證券者，以掛牌上市有價證券及銀行間債券市場為限 ，且投資前述有價證券總金額
不得超過該境外基金淨資產價值之百分之二十(境外基金機構依鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫向金管會申請並經認可者得適用鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫所列放寬前揭投資總金額上限至百分之四十之優惠措施 )；證券投資信託基金雖可投資
大陸地區之有價證券，惟仍應符合信託契約、公開說明書、證券投資信託基金管理辦法等相關規定及受到基金公司QFII額度之限制。又投資人須留意中國市場之政府政策、法令、會計稅務制度、經濟與市場等變動所可能產生之
投資風險。
14. 各基金於公開（含簡式）說明書或投資人須知揭露之風險報酬等級，係依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」而訂定，本公司亦依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」訂定基金產品風險等級。

Document 389: Open mouth operations (2026-11-06)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Open mouth operations (2026-11-06).pdf

Date: 2026-11-06

### Page 1

#australia #luciellis #rba
Open mouth operations
By Luci Ellis
Chief Economist Westpac Group, Westpac
13:34 October 23 2026 Share
The RBA does not want to promise what it will do, but it is increasingly trying to tell us what
it thinks.
Central banks’ communication is carefully scrutinised for every nuance. Lately, there seems to have
been more RBA communication to pore over. The method of delivery has also evolved. This has

### Page 2

Market participants pore over every utterance by central banks for clues to future rate moves. They will
have plenty to pore over in the run-up to the November RBA decision. After the flurry of fireside chats
this week, ’60 Minutes’ will run a pre-recorded interview with chief economist Sarah Hunter on Sunday.
Next Wednesday, the whole senior policy team will appear before Senate Estimates, just as the
quarterly CPI is released. (This timing will have been the Senate’s choice, not the RBA’s.) After the

decision will come the Statement on Monetary Policy, a media release and press conference. More
speeches will follow, including one by the Deputy Governor on the Thursday.
RBA communication does seem to have changed, not just following the RBA Review but also more
recently. There may be fewer policy meetings each year, but the communication outside that certainly
feels more frequent than a couple of years ago. Back in 2024, speaking engagements felt a little

sparse outside the post-meeting media conference and parliamentary appearances. We have counted
58 public engagements so far this year on the Speeches page on the RBA’s website. This compares
with 58 for the whole of last year and 52 in 2024. Excluding the ‘compulsory’ press conferences and
parliamentary appearances, fewer than half of the 2024 events were related to economics, markets
and monetary policy.
The ‘how’ has changed
The way the RBA communicates with markets has changed as well. There are fewer set-piece

speeches on policy and a lot more semi-scripted ‘fireside chats’. Compared with a few years ago, there
are more interviews across a wide range of media. This is a big change from the pre-Review world. It
was a huge deal when the previous Governor appeared on the 7:30 Report; it is hard to imagine him
agreeing to an Assistant Governor appearing on 60 Minutes.
Part of the change reflects increased efforts to reach a general audience. Central banks want to explain

why they do what they do. This matters especially when interest rates are rising but the household
sector is not the source of the inflation.
Speaking differently to different audiences can sometimes confuse markets. As the RBA’s Chief
Communication Officer highlighted in a recent panel discussion, the post-meeting media conference is
designed to speak to the public. Market participants and economists certainly tune in, but we are not
sometimes caused confusion when tone seems to vary. Different audiences call for different tone and

varying complexity.
The content has evolved, too. RBA officials are increasingly using speeches directed to professional
audiences to discuss more technical estimates of inferred variables such as neutral rates and
financial conditions. They are also highlighting that monetary policy combats inflation in more ways
than just squeezing mortgage holders.
One thing has not changed: the RBA is not in the business of providing ‘forward guidance’ promises

about the future path of interest rates. It does, however, give more running commentary about how it
is interpreting recent data. These interpretations are themselves useful signals about what the RBA
thinks it might do.

### Page 3

the primary audience. So if you think that what the Governor said in the media conference sounded
more ‘dovish’ than what another official says in a more market-oriented forum, it is partly because she
is trying to convey that the RBA understands that its recent policy actions are making things harder for
many Australians. But this is in much the same way that a doctor might say, “This might hurt a bit.” It
does, but that will not stop the treatment.

A related issue is that it is easier to control tone in pre-drafted material such as the minutes and set-
piece speeches than in unstructured Q&A. Senior central bankers are trained to be careful with their
words in unscripted settings. If the tone differs, the minutes are still more likely to say what the Board
really means than an off-the-cuff answer.
Greater communication with the broader public might have reduced the pressure to make the set-piece

speeches more accessible. Research on central bank communication often uses measures such as
the Flesch–Kincaid Grade Level and Flesch Reading Ease score. A comprehensive analysis was
beyond the scope of this note. However, a spot comparison suggests four speeches from 2026 (1, 2, 3,
4) were harder to read than four from 2021 (1, 2, 3, 4). The 2021 speeches scored in the 11 to 12
range, which is high-school-level complexity. The 2026 speeches scored between 12 and 15, which is

academic or technically dense text requiring university-level education. (Including two of my own 2021
speeches is an obvious caveat, but the two speeches by then-Governor Phil Lowe also scored better
on these metrics than the sample from this year. It should be noted, though, that readability metrics do
not necessarily imply better clarity if the concepts discussed are more abstract.)
The ‘what’ has changed
As noted above, the content of RBA communication has evolved, too. As its intellectual framework has

begun to put more weight on the balance of supply and demand, and those invisible ‘star’ variables
(NAIRUs, neutral real rates, ‘financial conditions’, etc.), the communication to professional audiences
has increasingly focused on those topics.
More recently, the RBA has also placed more emphasis on the full range of transmission channels from
interest rates to inflation (though not, as we have noted previously, the labour supply channel). This is

understandable in a world of supply shocks. People need to understand that monetary policy is not
simply an exercise in hitting mortgage borrowers. We doubt they would be comforted, though, by the
Deputy Governor’s explanation this week that the ‘cashflow channel’ was the smallest of the
transmission channels from interest rates to inflation, with the exchange rate much more prominent.
That implies a lot of pain for a small group, for little effect on inflation. It raises the question of whether

other policy tools or institutional arrangements might get the same effect on inflation through the other
channels without hitting a minority of households disproportionately.
We have also seen a move away from using catchphrases to summarise the position. From ‘narrow
path’ to ‘not ruling anything in or out’ to ‘cautious and predictable’, once entrenched, a phrase can
outlast its usefulness.

### Page 4

Related articles
One thing that has not changed is the way the minutes record the presentations to the Board and the
members’ discussion. The minutes rarely convey much new information beyond the post-meeting
statements. Sometimes, though, the minutes reveal the Board’s reaction to the staff’s analysis. The
August 2024 minutes were a good example: the discussion highlighted just how sensitive the forecasts
were to key judgements by the staff.
The ‘why’ has not changed

Despite these changes, the underlying objectives of the RBA’s communication have not changed. It
wants to explain – and perhaps justify – its decisions. And it wants to describe how it interprets
incoming data, especially inflation, the labour market and the balance of supply and demand.
The RBA, like any central bank, is not really in the business of providing ‘forward guidance’ about
future moves. Circumstances change, so it can never guarantee a particular future path for interest

rates. Some academic literature argues that promising to keep rates low for a long time (or high, for
that matter) independently amplifies the transmission of monetary policy beyond the effect of current-
period interest rates alone, by shifting the short end of the yield curve. Central banks including the RBA
have certainly tried jawboning in this way from time to time. The prevailing view, though, is that the
potential cost of reputational loss if things do not turn out as predicted outweighs the benefits of a

slightly stronger transmission mechanism.
In this context, it is the interpretation of data that provides the signal about future policy directions. This
is why it was noteworthy that the Governor still characterised the labour market as ‘a little tight’ after the
September meeting, even though the Board’s post-meeting communication did not. We have been
noting for some time that the RBA staff have a downbeat view of supply capacity. Together with the

RBA’s oft-stated concerns about inflation, this is why the Deputy Governor’s ‘hawkishness’ did not
surprise us.

Persistent energy pressures have further to run
19 October 2026
Key points
• Shipping disruptions, depleted
inventories and elevated freight
costs are keeping physical crude
and fuel markets tight despite a

reported recovery in Gulf exports.
• Brent is forecast to average
US$101/bbl in Q4 before easing
to US$77/bbl in Q4 2027.
• Refined product markets are
expected to ease more slowly than
crude, with elevated gasoil cracks,
shipping and insurance costs slowing
declines in petrol and diesel prices.
• Under an escalation scenario,
renewed conflict and deeper supply
disruptions could push Brent towards
US$150/bbl in H1 2027, prolonging
elevated fuel and LNG prices.

• Under an early resolution scenario, a
durable US-Iran agreement accelerates
supply recovery, taking Brent towards
US$65/bbl by Q4 2027 and bringing
fuel and LNG prices down faster.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
Low inventories and higher shipping costs will keep oil and
fuel prices elevated, with Brent futures forecast to average
US$101/bbl in Q4 before easing to US$77/bbl by Q4 2027
as supply recovers. Refined fuels and LNG prices should

ease more slowly. Risks are two-sided: escalation could
push Brent towards US$150/bbl, while an early US-Iran
agreement could see a faster normalisation in supply with
prices returning close to pre-conflict levels by end-2027.
This article was first published in the October Market Outlook .
Brent crude is back above US$100/bbl on fading confidence
in a lasting US-Iran agreement and intermittent attacks on
shipping. Buffers remain depleted, with the IEA estimating

global observed inventories were 507mb lower at the end of
August than at the start of the conflict.
This comes despite reported crude exports through the Strait of
Hormuz recovering to around 70% to 90% of pre-conflict levels
in late September. Saudi and UAE exports through the Yanbu and
Fujairah pipelines continue, with the East-West pipeline back to
normal after the early September attacks. Tanker crossings have
risen, although transponders remain switched off, leaving visible

transit at 1% to 4% of pre-conflict levels, while cargo transfers in
the Gulf of Oman have increased as an alternative route.
However, these workarounds are slower and more costly. Ship-
to-ship transfers can take 5 to 10 days, US escorts through
the Gulf via a route close to the Oman shoreline cause delays
while some tankers are taking longer routes. Fewer ships are
also available, with many redeployed to other markets. Higher
insurance premiums are adding to costs, with Gulf-to-China

tanker rates above US$0.9mn a day, around 15x higher than a
year ago. With prompt barrels (available for physical delivery)
scarce, the gap between physical and futures prices widened
again, with Dated Brent around US$136/bbl in early October
against US$101/bbl for front-month futures.
Oil and fuel prices push higher ...
Refined prices have risen more than crude
Refined product markets were already tight going into the
conflict. Reduced Gulf supply, exacerbated by Ukrainian strikes

on Russian refineries, has added to that tightness. Together
with higher shipping costs and rising Dated Brent prices,
Singapore Gasoil 10ppm (diesel) has roughly doubled since the
Strait closed, compared with around 40% for crude futures.
Consequently, Singapore gasoil crack spread widened
to an average of around US$36.1/bbl in early October,
from US$17.5/bbl across 2025. Alongside the Australian
government’s excise tax returning to normal, after it had been

halved, this has lifted national diesel and petrol prices to
around A$2.83/L and A$2.41/L.
Outlook
We do not see a lasting US-Iran agreement until at least
mid-2027. In the meantime, we assume there will not be a
return to full-scale military intervention. However, periodic
Iranian attacks on ships and facilities are expected to continue,
as will the US blockade of Iranian exports, keeping a supply
risk premium in place. We look for Brent futures to average

around US$101/bbl in Q4, then ease gradually as demand
from refiners and governments to rebuild stocks partly offsets
recovering supply. As the premium fades and the market
moves into surplus in late 2027, we forecast Brent at around
US$77/bbl in Q4 2027, easing towards US$70/bbl in 2028 as
Middle East production fully recovers.
Oil balance to gradually improve over 2027
Global oil supply is expected to rise by 4.0mb/d (4.0%) to
103.7mb/d in 2027, following a 6.1% fall in 2026. Most of

this reflects a gradual recovery in Gulf output, with Houthi
Sian Fenner, Head of Business & Industry Economics
Justin Smirk, Senior Economist
Luka Belobajdic, Economist
